# Testing — Portty Regression Suite

**Maintained by:** the team (update this file as part of every phase that adds or removes tests)
**Canonical home:** this file at the repo root (`TESTING.md`) supersedes the scattered per-phase UAT logs as the living document going forward. The per-phase logs (`137-HUMAN-UAT.md`, `138-HUMAN-UAT.md`, `141-HUMAN-UAT.md`, and per-phase `*-VERIFICATION.md` files) remain in place as historical record and are not deleted.

---

## 1. Overview

This document is the single source of truth for Portty's regression test program. It holds:

1. **Suite Manifest** — the four test groups, their file counts, run commands, and what each guards
2. **Merge Gate** — the exact `gh api` command to apply (and roll back) branch protection on `main`
3. **Requirement→Test Traceability Map** — v4.0-scoped, hand-maintained table mapping v4.0 requirement IDs to specific test files
4. **Manual Regression Checklist** — human-intervention behaviors that cannot be automated
5. **Standing Convention** — the per-phase rule every executor must follow

The traceability table is machine-validated by `tests/check-traceability-paths.sh`, which runs in CI as part of the `build (portty, darwin/universal, macos-latest)` job and exits 1 if any mapped path no longer exists on disk.

---

## 2. Suite Manifest

The entire CI suite IS the regression suite. No build tags, no relocated files — the suite is delineated here by manifest only.

| Group | Count | Location | Run Command | Guards |
|-------|-------|----------|-------------|--------|
| Go unit/integration | **134** `*_test.go` files | `internal/`, repo root | `GOTOOLCHAIN=go1.26.11 go test -race -short ./...` | Daemon API, relay wire framing, capability model, PTY, webserver, status, tailnet; security fuzz targets (seed corpora run as tests) |
| vitest (frontend) | **90** `*.test.ts/tsx` files | `frontend/src/` | `cd frontend && pnpm test` | React component render contracts (sidebar, tabs, toolbar, dialogs, Settings sheet, banners), UI state, CSS token source gates (no literal colour outside the token blocks), lib adapters (relay, remote, sidebar peers) |
| Playwright e2e | **7** `*.spec.ts` files | `frontend/e2e/` | `cd frontend && pnpm exec playwright test` | Web surface: CSP, web-links toggle, plugin hot-swap, vendored xterm addons, web-share guest scope (the guest sees only the terminal: WEBCHAT-03, CHAT-01), live light/dark (THEM-01) |
| build-script | **1** `build-script.test.sh` | `tests/` | `bash tests/build-script.test.sh` | Go build + Wails asset embedding |
| **Total** | **232** | — | — | — |

> **Toolchain:** Go commands in this repo run with `GOTOOLCHAIN=go1.26.11`, the `toolchain` line in go.mod. With a newer Go installed, `GOTOOLCHAIN=auto` never downgrades to the pin, so set it by hand (`build.sh` exports it from go.mod unless the caller already set one). The root package refuses to compile under Go 1.27 (`toolchain_guard.go`, build tag `go1.27`) because tailscale.com v1.98.3's JSON decoding breaks with Go 1.27's encoding/json (tailcfg's `format:nano` tag), a run-time failure the compiler would not catch. When tailscale.com is upgraded, run `TestTailscaleJSON_FormatTagCanary` under the new Go first, then remove the go.mod toolchain line and toolchain_guard.go together.

Production changes: live frames during a replay are held per viewer (capped at the 4 MiB history size) instead of filling the 256-frame queue; the resume offset survives a socket the proxy accepts and then refuses; GUI, browser and CLI count as connected only at the host's first frame; Quit All and app shutdown stop the respawn poller; the daemon error is atomic and clears with a `daemon:recovered` event once a respawned daemon stays up; the daemon holds an exclusive flock on `daemon.lock` next to the socket; the browser probe and each reconnect socket time out into the normal backoff. Section 4: SHAR-07 rows extended for those files, plus new rows for App.daemon-recovered.test.tsx and internal/daemon/process_test.go. > Note: Portty Phase 5 gap closure (05-08..05-16, SHAR-07/VERF-01; reconciled by 05-15): counts from `git ls-files` — Go **123** (+5 since the Phase 5 note: internal/relay/replay_fidelity_test.go (05-08), internal/wakewatch/wakewatch_test.go (05-10), internal/webserver/tailscale_json_test.go (05-11), app_wake_test.go (05-13), app_respawn_test.go (05-16)), vitest **86** (+3: frontend/src/components/__tests__/ErrorBoundary.test.tsx (05-09), frontend/src/lib/__tests__/replayFidelity.test.ts (05-12), frontend/src/components/__tests__/App.remote-poll.test.tsx (05-13)), Playwright **7**, build-script **1**, total **217**. The per-plan notes below kept the Go row for this reconciliation. Edited in place: internal/webserver/resume_test.go (`TestWSS_InputAfterWrappedReplay`, 05-08); internal/attach/loop_test.go (`TestRun_WakeSkipsBackoff`, `TestRun_WakeMinGap`, `TestRun_WakeProbesOpenSocket`, `TestRun_WakeHealthySocketKept`, `TestRun_WakeNoPongHostKept`, `TestRun_WakeStaleInputNeverSent`, 05-10); internal/daemon/attach_viewers_test.go (`TestCLIAttach_LongOutputDuringGap`, 05-10); tests/build-script.test.sh (toolchain pin checks, 05-11); cmd_attach_test.go (`TestRemoteReauth_DeadDaemonRunsEnsure`, `TestRemoteReauth_HealthyNotSharingSkipsEnsure`, 05-16); frontend/src/components/__tests__/App.open-remote.test.tsx and frontend/src/lib/__tests__/tabs.test.ts (duplicate tabs, 05-09); TerminalPanel.reconnect, TerminalPanel.hot-swap and TerminalPanel.scale tests (ordered reset, resize and scrollbar, 05-12); frontend/src/lib/relayClient.test.ts (wake and constructor-throw cases, 05-13); frontend/src/components/__tests__/App.nav.test.tsx (relay port refresh, 05-16); frontend/src/components/__tests__/appHarness.ts (keeps EventsOn callbacks, 05-13; not a counted file). Production changes, one each:
> - Geometry-aware replay (05-08): the hub never sends a delta or raw replay that reaches back past the last host resize, a rendered replay never restores mode 2026, and a cursor in pending wrap re-prints its last-column cell.
> - Ordered guest reset and resize (05-12): a full replay resets xterm with an in-band RIS in stream order, and a guest resize waits for the queued writes before it resizes and rescales.
> - Guest scrollbar stretch (05-12): the guest element is stretched to the pane so the scrollbar sits at the pane edge.
> - Duplicate-tab guards and ErrorBoundary (05-09): a double click during a slow connect opens one tab, closing a tab never activates itself, and a crashing pane shows fixed fallback copy instead of a blank window.
> - Wake-driven reconnect (05-10, 05-13): `internal/wakewatch` (wall-clock tick gap or a changed interface address list) makes the CLI, the GUI tab (through the `system:wake` event) and the browser view probe an open socket or skip a pending backoff at once.
> - RelayClient constructor-throw hardening (05-13): a throwing WebSocket constructor is a failed attempt in reconnect mode and never throws out of a method or logs the URL.
> - Toolchain pin and compile guard (05-11): see the Toolchain note above.
> - Viewing-Mac daemon respawn (05-16): see the 05-16 note.
>

> Note: Test isolation fix (2026-11-16): `testmain_test.go` (root package) gives every root test a temporary HOME, and `daemon.EnsureDaemon` refuses to spawn a detached daemon from a test binary (`testing.Testing()`). Before this, root tests reached `EnsureDaemon`, re-ran the test binary as `portty.test daemon` in the user's real `~/Library/Application Support/portty`, and left it running holding the real socket. Go test-file count +1 (`testmain_test.go`).

> Note: Portty Phase 1 plan 01-09 (CHAT-01 inject bridge removed, STRP-04 security fuzz harness): counts from `git ls-files` — Go **122** (+2: `internal/relay/fuzz_test.go`, `internal/webserver/fuzz_test.go`), vitest **125**, Playwright **7**, build-script **1**, total **255**. **Security fuzz harness:** `capability.FuzzVerify` (`internal/capability/capability_fuzz_test.go`) plus five new targets — `FuzzParseFrame`, `FuzzInputAbsorberFilter`, `FuzzSanitizeChatContent` (relay) and `FuzzRequireCapability`, `FuzzWebServerRoutes` (webserver). Their seed corpora run in the normal `go test` suite; for a real fuzz pass run each target alone for 10 s (`go test` allows one `-fuzz` pattern per run): `go test -run='^$' -fuzz='^FuzzParseFrame$' -fuzztime=10s ./internal/relay` (same for `FuzzInputAbsorberFilter`, `FuzzSanitizeChatContent`), `go test -run='^$' -fuzz='^FuzzRequireCapability$' -fuzztime=10s ./internal/webserver` (same for `FuzzWebServerRoutes`), `go test -run='^$' -fuzz='^FuzzVerify$' -fuzztime=10s ./internal/capability`. `FuzzWebServerRoutes` found a 500 on `/assets/<invalid UTF-8>` (fixed: `assetsNoStore` returns 404); the crasher is kept in `internal/webserver/testdata/fuzz/FuzzWebServerRoutes/` as a regression seed. Retargeted in place: `internal/relay/server_inject_test.go` and `internal/webserver/inject_test.go` now prove a raw 0x35 frame (the retired inject frame) from RO and RW caps writes zero PTY bytes and a following chat send still works; `TestHandleChatSend_ROCanPostInjectStillGated` → `TestHandleChatSend_ROCanPost_NoPTYWrite`; `TestSanitizePTYText` replaced by `TestSanitizeChatContent`; new `TestChatStore_LegacySessionInjectLine`; inject cases removed from `ChatMessage.test.tsx`, `relayClient.test.ts`, `ChatPanel.test.tsx`, `MentionPopover.test.tsx`; `chat-parity.spec.ts` lost the SC-4 inject-indicator test and the ROCHAT-02 half.

> Note: Phase 169-01 RE-EXECUTED (FIX-05, #120, v4.2 — honest permission-aware Tailscale detection, superseding the invalidated CLI-invocation approach): no new test files — EXTENDED `internal/webserver/tailscale_test.go` in place (Go count stays 374, Total stays 526 at that point in the phase). CR-01 proved the reverted CLI-invocation approach (shelled out to `tailscale status --json`) hit the identical per-user macsys permission gate as the SDK path and could report a FALSE Connected, so it was fully reverted: `cliStatusFunc`/`runTailscaleStatusCLI`/`realCLIStatusFunc` and the `os/exec`/`encoding/json` imports are removed from `tailscale.go`, and the three `TestCheckHealth_CLIFallback_*` tests are removed. Two new tests replace them: `TestCheckHealth_PermissionLimited` (SC1: SDK read error + injected `perm` probe reports macsys-permission-limited → `PermissionLimited=true`, `Connected=false` (hard SC — no false Connected), `HasCerts=false`, `IP=""`), `TestCheckHealth_DaemonDown_NotPermissionLimited` (D-02: SDK read error + injected `perm` probe reports false → `PermissionLimited=false`, `DaemonUp=false`, `Connected=false`, unchanged from today's daemon-down behavior). `TestCheckHealth_FullyHealthy` stays green unchanged, proving the SDK-success path (Steps 3-4) is untouched (D-06/SC2). Implementation: `internal/webserver/tailscale.go` — new `permProbeFunc` injectable type mirroring `statusFunc`/`prefsFunc`; `realPermProbe()` (darwin-only; globs `/Library/Tailscale/sameuserproof-*`, `os.Open`s each match to observe `fs.ErrPermission` without ever reading file contents — T-169-02 info-disclosure boundary — then confirms liveness via `macsysDaemonAlive`); `macsysDaemonAlive()` (reads the `ipnport` symlink, parses strictly as an integer port — never a filesystem path, T-169-01 — and TCP-dials `127.0.0.1:<port>` with a 1s timeout); additive `PermissionLimited bool` field (`json:"permissionLimited"`) on `TailscaleHealth`; `checkHealth`'s `err != nil` arm sets `PermissionLimited`/`DaemonUp`/`Installed=true` only via the injected `perm` probe, leaving `Connected`/`HasCerts`/`IP`/`Domain`/`AcceptDNS` at zero values; `CheckHealth`/`CheckHealthWithCustomPath` wire `realPermProbe()`.

> Note: Phase 165-05 gap-closure (FNL-03, loopback-HTTP fix — 165-04 Option A https+insecure proven dead live): No new files — test rewritten in existing `internal/webserver/funnel_test.go` (TestEnableFunnel_ProxyTargetReachable); implementation in `internal/webserver/server.go`. Root cause confirmed via live off-tailnet M-34: tailscaled dials the raw tailnet IP on hop 2 and sends SNI=IP-literal; the daemon's HTTPS listener's SNI-driven cert (ws.lc.GetCertificate) has no cert for an IP literal → TLS internal_error → HTTP 502; https+insecure only disables the CLIENT's cert-hostname verification, not the SERVER's SNI-based cert selection. The sibling target https+insecure://<FQDN>:443 is also dead: public DNS resolves the .ts.net FQDN to Tailscale's Funnel ingress IPs → Funnel→ingress→Funnel loop → hang. FIX: added a PLAIN-HTTP listener bound to loopback (127.0.0.1, ephemeral port) in startTailscale, serving the same ws.mux; EnableFunnel now sets Proxy = `http://127.0.0.1:<loopbackPort>` (no TLS on hop 2 — the traffic never leaves the host, kernel loopback only). The 165-04 TestEnableFunnel_ProxyTargetReachable was FALSE-GREEN (a loopback self-signed cert answered any SNI so the cert-selection failure never surfaced); rewritten to assert scheme==http (rejects https/https+insecure as 165-04 regression), host==127.0.0.1, port==loopback-listener-port!=TLS-listener-port, AND dials with a PLAIN http.Client requiring a served response. Counts unchanged (no new files).
>
> Note: Phase 165-04 gap-closure (FNL-03/FNL-05, live-UAT defects): No new files — tests added to existing files. `internal/webserver/funnel_test.go` +TestEnableFunnel_ProxyTargetReachable (FNL-03 502 regression guard: asserts Proxy = https+insecure://<bindIP>:<port> — NOT https://localhost:<port> — AND that the proxy target is actually reachable via InsecureSkipVerify HTTPS client; catches the exact 502 class). `internal/daemon/funnel_test.go` +TestFunnelTeardown_KillPath (FNL-05 kill-path teardown: two sub-cases — single-session DELETE clears fake config and FunnelActive, two-session ref-count kill A leaves B up then B's cleanup clears config). Implementation: `internal/webserver/server.go` EnableFunnel Proxy target `https://localhost:<port>` → `https+insecure://<ws.config.BindIP>:<port>` (Option A locked decision); `internal/daemon/api.go` handleDeleteSession calls a.runSessionExitCleanup(id) synchronously after KillSession (no 10s grace). Counts unchanged (no new files).

### CI Workflow Mapping

- `build.yml` (runs on push + PR): a single macOS job (`macos-latest`) running the brand gate (`tests/check-brand.sh`), the share removal gate (`tests/check-share-removal.sh`), Go tests (race detector), build-script tests, vitest, the traceability path-check step and `scripts/verify-icons.sh`, then the Wails build
- `e2e.yml` (runs on push/PR to `main`): Playwright cross-browser (chromium, firefox, webkit)
- `release.yml` (runs on `v*` tags): builds, signs and notarizes Portty.app, then notarizes and staples the hdiutil DMG and checks it like the in-app updater (codesign, Team S2K7P43927, spctl) before publishing it plus checksums. There is no distribute workflow.

## 3. Merge Gate: How to Apply Branch Protection

Branch protection requires both CI check contexts to pass before a PR can merge. Admin direct push to `main` is allowed (preserves the GSD `.planning/` doc-commit flow). No PR review requirement.

### Apply Protection

CI is currently disabled on the repository; the user must re-apply this protection (with the two contexts below) when CI is re-enabled.

```bash
gh api repos/scottkw/portty/branches/main/protection \
  --method PUT \
  --header "Accept: application/vnd.github+json" \
  --input - <<'EOF'
{
  "required_status_checks": {
    "strict": false,
    "checks": [
      {"context": "build (portty, darwin/universal, macos-latest)", "app_id": 15368},
      {"context": "playwright", "app_id": 15368}
    ]
  },
  "enforce_admins": false,
  "required_pull_request_reviews": null,
  "restrictions": null
}
EOF
```

**Key field rationale:**
- `checks[]` with `app_id` — current GitHub format (`contexts[]` is deprecated)
- `strict: false` — branches do not need to be up-to-date with `main` before merge
- `enforce_admins: false` — admin (scottkw) can push directly to `main` without CI; this preserves the GSD `.planning/` doc-commit flow
- `required_pull_request_reviews: null` — no PR review requirement
- `restrictions: null` — no user/team push restrictions
- GitHub Actions `app_id: 15368` — verified 2026-06-21 from live check-runs API

### Roll Back Protection

```bash
gh api repos/scottkw/portty/branches/main/protection --method DELETE
```

### Maintenance Note (Pitfall-1)

**If `build.yml` matrix entries change** (platform added, OS version bumped, label renamed), the status check context names change and the branch protection rule silently stops enforcing the renamed check. After any matrix change: roll back with DELETE, then re-apply with PUT using the new check context names from `gh api repos/scottkw/portty/commits/main/check-runs`.

**Warning sign:** A new PR passes CI but the "required checks" status badge shows an unknown check name.

Portty 03-06 renamed the build.yml matrix entry, so the required context changed. Re-apply protection with the command above when CI is re-enabled (a manual step for the user; branch protection on a private repository may need a paid plan).

## 5. Manual Regression Checklist

Human-intervention items that cannot be automated. Run before each tagged release.

> Retired: Portty Phase 4 (SHAR-05) removed public internet sharing, its consent UI and code-based access, so Categories I, X and Y (items M-34..M-36, M-46 and M-47) are retired.

> Retired: Portty 04-05 (SHAR-01) replaced the tabbed share dialog with the one-switch Share popover, so Category A (item M-01) is retired; Category AR (M-81) checks the popover.

> Retired: Portty 04-05 removed the share reminder and its Settings switch (Category J, M-16) and the public-sharing GUI (Category T, M-37..M-40). M-40's Tailscale-off check carries on as M-82 in Category AR.

> Retired: Portty 04-06 (SHAR-01) opens peer tabs with no prompt, so Category G (M-13) is retired; its two-session isolation check moved into M-04.

### Category B — Sidebar Peer Attach (NAV-02)

GUI remote attach starts from sidebar peer rows (Portty 02-04). Setup for all items: the built app on this Mac with Tailscale connected, and a second Mac on the same tailnet sharing one session.

- **M-03** First open: the second Mac's short hostname appears as a sidebar group with its shared session as a row. One click opens a `<host> › <session>` tab at once (no prompt of any kind), it streams the session with full control, and its sidebar row shows as selected. With the second Mac asleep or on another Tailscale account, the click still opens the tab, showing `Couldn't connect to <host>. Check that it's awake and signed in to your Tailscale account.`; clicking the row again after waking it connects.
  - _Why not automatable:_ needs a live reachable peer on a second machine. The routing, the connect-failed banner and the retry are render-tested in `App.open-remote.test.tsx` with a faked bridge.
  - _Source:_ 02-04-PLAN.md Task 3 human-check; 04-06-PLAN.md Task 1 (04-UI-SPEC Peer sidebar and peer tabs).
- **M-04** Reopen and isolation: close the peer tab, then click the same peer row again; the tab reopens at once with no prompt. Clicking the row while its tab is open just activates that tab. With two sessions shared from the second Mac, open both: two independent `<host> › <session>` tabs, each streaming its own session. Turn Share off on the second Mac with a tab open: the tab keeps its scrollback, stops taking input and shows `<host> stopped sharing this session.`, and its sidebar row disappears at once.
  - _Why not automatable:_ needs the live peer and the daemon's remote capability store.
  - _Source:_ 02-04-PLAN.md Task 3; Phase 168-03 two-session isolation (was M-13); 04-06-PLAN.md Task 1.
- **M-05** Peer states: put the second Mac to sleep (or quit its app); within 30 s its group shows "Can't reach this Mac. Check that it's awake and on your tailnet." Turn off sharing on it: its group disappears and, with no other peer sharing, "Other Macs" reads "No shared sessions on other Macs." Disconnect Tailscale on this Mac: the peer area reads "Peers appear when Tailscale is connected." and the footer "Tailscale not connected"; open peer tabs stay.
  - _Why not automatable:_ needs a live tailnet and a second Mac.
  - _Source:_ 02-UI-SPEC.md "Peer group states".
- **M-70** Side by side and peer discovery on port 7444 (Portty 03-02). Needs two Macs on one tailnet.
  1. On Mac A, run the upstream app and Portty at the same time: both launch, Portty's daemon socket is under ~/Library/Application Support/portty and its web server listens on 7444 (`lsof -nP -iTCP:7444 -sTCP:LISTEN` shows the Portty daemon).
  2. On Mac B (Portty build), Other Macs lists Mac A's Portty host and its shared session, and does not list a host that runs only the upstream app.
  3. `curl -sk -D - https://<macA>.<tailnet>.ts.net:7444/api/sessions -o /dev/null` shows `HTTP/2 401` and `x-portty: 1`.
  - _Why not automatable:_ two Macs and a live tailnet. The probe and marker logic is unit-tested in `internal/tailnet/tailnet_test.go` and `internal/webserver/probe_marker_test.go`.
  - _Source:_ 03-02-PLAN.md Task 3 human-check.

### Category C — Terminal / Theme (POL-04)

- **M-07** Terminal repaints cleanly when macOS switches Light/Dark on an active session; a hidden tab shows the new palette as soon as it is selected; after a tab switch away and back; CMD+/- font resize does not garble the output.
  - _Why not automatable:_ Wails native webview required for the GPU atlas paint path; Playwright cannot exercise the native rendering path.
  - _Source:_ 142-VERIFICATION.md (PASS recorded 2026-06-21; standing check for each release)
- **M-54** New Session modal (Portty 01-04/01-11): the modal shows only the Working Directory picker (no Shell line, no helper text); with a long working directory chosen the path truncates with an ellipsis on one line and the modal stays 460px wide. Create Session opens a tab whose shell answers `[[ -o login ]] && echo login; echo $ZSH_VERSION` with `login` and a zsh version, even when the account login shell is bash (`chsh -s /bin/bash`). Stop any older Portty daemon first (a stale daemon still expects a `cli` field).
  - _Why not automatable:_ Text truncation and visual hierarchy need a rendered native webview; jsdom does no layout.
  - _Source:_ 01-04-PLAN.md Task 2 human-check
- **M-55** TERM-05 tab closes on every shell exit (Portty 01-10): in the built app, open three sessions; in one type `exit`, in another `exit 3`, and in the third run `sleep 400; exit 0` and wait about 7 minutes. Each tab closes immediately on exit, including the one that lived more than 5 minutes. No toast, countdown, keep-open control or error tab appears, tabs show no status indicator, the quit dialog lists session names with no status indicator or text, and Settings has no auto-close toggle.
  - _Why not automatable:_ needs the native webview, a live daemon and real wall-clock time past 5 minutes. The watcher logic is covered by `app_exit_test.go`, the handler by `App.shellExit.test.tsx`.
  - _Source:_ 01-10-PLAN.md Task 2 human-check
- **M-56** TERM-02 sessions survive hiding and relaunching the GUI (Portty 01-10): open two sessions, run `sleep 20; exit` in one and print some output in the other, choose "Quit GUI only" at once (hide to tray), wait 30 s and reopen the window from the tray; then quit the GUI and relaunch the app from Finder. Each time the running session is present with its scrollback, and the session that exited while hidden is gone.
  - _Why not automatable:_ needs the tray, a Finder relaunch and a live daemon. The relaunch logic on the same daemon is covered by `TestExitWatcher_GUIRelaunchKeepsRunningSessions`.
  - _Source:_ 01-10-PLAN.md Task 2 human-check

### Category D — Signed Build / Distribution

- **M-08** AirDrop'd signed macOS build passes Gatekeeper (`spctl --assess --verbose /Applications/Portty.app`) on a separate machine without quarantine prompts.
  - _Why not automatable:_ Requires code signing and two physical machines.
  - _Source:_ General release protocol; macOS signing cert shared across repos (re-exported 2026-04-08)

### Category P — Terminal Screen-Share Garble (Issue #109, Phase 157)

The VIEW-01..05 behavioral proof for the web guest and the desktop guest relies on live multi-window rendering that vitest/Playwright cannot exercise. **Reconciled Phase 175-07 (v4.2):** since the Phase 159 `/sessions/{id}` → `/app/` redirect, the reachable web guest surface is the `/app/` React SPA (`WebShareSessionView.tsx` → `TerminalPanel.tsx`, real xterm.js) — the standalone vendored `web/assets/terminal.js` viewer this category originally described (Phase 157) is now **dead code**, unreachable by any live share URL. The items below are the manual complement required by ROADMAP success criterion 6.

- **M-27** Issue #109 two-surface garble check — host PTY + smaller-windowed web guest, no garble:
  1. Start a local session as host. Enable web-share and copy the RW share URL.
  2. Open the share URL in a browser window **smaller** than the host terminal (e.g. resize the browser to 80 columns × 24 rows while the host runs at 120 × 30 or wider). The URL redirects (Phase 159) to `/app/?session=…&cap=…`.
  3. Run a full-screen TUI in the host PTY (e.g. `htop` or `vim`).
  4. **Confirm guest:** no overlapping or doubled characters (no "garble" as shown in Issue #109 screenshot). The guest terminal is downscaled via CSS `transform: scale(...)` so the 120×30 grid fits the 80×24 viewport — no character wrapping or line-overrun.
  5. **Confirm host:** the host PTY grid (cols/rows) is unchanged by the guest window size. `echo ${COLUMNS}×${LINES}` in the host PTY reports the host-window dimensions, not the guest's.
  6. **Confirm downscale cap:** resize the browser guest window to be LARGER than the host terminal. Confirm the guest shows no upscale (the terminal does not stretch beyond its natural pixel size; `scale(s)` has `s ≤ 1.0`); excess space is blank padding.
  - _Why not automatable:_ Live multi-window xterm.js render required; `transform: scale(...)` pixel output is not unit-assertable. The reachable web guest surface is the `/app/` React SPA (`TerminalPanel.tsx`, real xterm.js) since the Phase 159 redirect; `web/assets/terminal.js` is dead code and is no longer the viewer under test here (reconciled Phase 175-07 — see also the BUG-01 readability-floor extension to this same scale pipeline in M-48/Category Z).
  - _Source:_ Phase 157 Issue #109; 157-VALIDATION.md Manual-Only Verifications row 1 + row 2; VIEW-01..05 criteria 1 and 4.

- **M-28** Cross-surface parity — desktop guest scale matches web guest, no PTY interference:
  1. With the same session running (from M-27 or a fresh one), open the session in the desktop app as a **remote guest** via its sidebar peer row (Category B; not as the host owner). This exercises the `isGuest = remote || !!wsURL` path in `TerminalPanel.tsx`.
  2. Confirm the desktop guest panel shows the same no-garble scaled view as the web guest: the host's PTY grid is applied via `term.resize(cols, rows)` then `transform: scale(s)` where `s ≤ 1.0`.
  3. Confirm the desktop guest **never** sends a `sendResize` back to the server (the host PTY cols/rows must not change when the desktop guest panel resizes its window).
  4. Confirm `transform-origin: top left` is in effect: the scaled terminal is anchored to the top-left corner, not centered.
  - _Why not automatable:_ Native Wails WebView is not accessible to Playwright. Cross-surface parity (desktop vs web guest) is release-blocking per the standing parity policy.
  - _Source:_ Phase 157 157-VALIDATION.md Manual-Only Verifications row 3; VIEW-04/05 criterion 5 (cross-surface parity gate).

### Category U — Native Notifications (NTF)

- **M-41** Native notification path (Portty 01-11; NTF-01): the awaiting-input toggle is gone (CONTEXT Amendment 2), and `sendNotification` (`notification_darwin.go`, called through `App.sendNotificationFunc`) is the one kept native path. Update notices use it (M-92, M-93).
  1. Use a Developer-ID-signed Portty build (`wails build -tags wailsassets` + `codesign --options runtime --entitlements build/entitlements.plist`).
  2. Open Settings → Behavior and confirm it shows only "Start minimized to system tray" (no awaiting-input toggle, no notification-permission hint).
  3. With at least one session open, choose "Quit GUI only" from the quit dialog.
  4. Confirm exactly one native notification, attributed to Portty, says "Portty is still running in the menu bar." (no open sessions), "Portty is still running in the menu bar with 1 session open." or "Portty is still running in the menu bar with N sessions open.", and the tray icon stays. The first send may show the macOS permission prompt (authorization is requested lazily).
  - _Why not automatable:_ native `UNUserNotificationCenter` delivery needs a signed bundle and a live desktop session. The unbundled guard is unit-tested (`TestHasAppBundleID_FalseWhenUnbundled`, `TestSendNotification_NoBundleReturnsCleanly` in `notification_darwin_test.go`).
  - _Source:_ Phase 167 NTF-01 (history: the 167-05/06 crash and delivery fixes); rewritten for Portty 01-11.

### Category V — Bug Fix & Settings Polish (FIX-01, FIX-02)

> Portty 02-11: Settings is a sheet (⌘,) with General / Sharing / Security panes. Plugin settings live in General ▸ Terminal Plugins, the Tailscale status and a short explanation in Sharing (Portty 04-05), and Certificate Transparency and signing-key regeneration in Security.

- **M-42** Live two-browser-viewer smoke test (FIX-02, #117; Portty 04-05: the owner's force-disconnect button left with the share dialog, so turning Share off is the way to drop viewers).
  1. On the owner's machine, start a session, turn Share on from the toolbar popover and copy the link.
  2. Open the link in TWO separate real browser clients (two browser profiles, or a normal and a private window) so each is a distinct WebSocket connection.
  3. Confirm BOTH viewers show the live terminal stream at once — neither is closed when the second connects (regression guard for #117 Part A; `TestHub_TwoWebOriginSubscribers_NoEviction`).
  4. Turn Share off in the popover. Confirm BOTH browser clients stop taking input within one poll cycle.
  - _Why not automatable:_ requires two independent WebSocket clients against a live daemon; no Playwright spec opens two live viewers against the daemon relay.
  - _Source:_ Phase 168-06 (#117, v4.2) + `TestHub_TwoWebOriginSubscribers_NoEviction`/`TestRevokeWebViewers` traceability rows (FIX-02).

- **M-43** Live browser-console CSP/hot-swap check (FIX-01, #112): open the `/app/` share URL in a real browser, change plugin-config, confirm live hot-swap without reload, and inspect DevTools Console for CSP errors.
  1. On the owner's machine, start a session, enable web sharing, and obtain the `/app/?session=&cap=` share URL (the SPA surface, not the retired raw `/sessions/{id}` viewer).
  2. Open that URL in a real desktop browser (plain Chrome/Firefox, NOT the Wails native webview — DevTools Console is required). Open the browser's DevTools Console before navigating.
  3. Confirm the terminal renders using the daemon's current plugin config (fonts as configured), self-fetched via `GET /api/plugin-config?cap=` (no Wails-provided `pluginConfig` prop reaches a web guest — Phase 168-02).
  4. On the owner's machine (desktop app), open Settings (⌘,) ▸ General ▸ Terminal Plugins and change a plugin-config setting, then click Save Plugins (e.g. terminal font size) that is normally pushed live to connected viewers.
  5. Confirm the browser tab picks up the change WITHOUT a page reload — the EventSource `plugin-config` SSE stream applies the pushed update in place (Phase 168-02 hot-swap).
  6. Inspect the DevTools Console for CSP (`Content-Security-Policy`) violation errors during steps 2-5. **Note the documented caveat (RESEARCH Pitfall 1 / T-168-03, tracked in upstream issue #123):** the `/app/` SPA route currently has NO CSP header at all (unlike the raw `/sessions/{id}` viewer, which does), so this step's expected outcome is "no CSP errors because no CSP is enforced yet" — record whether any errors appear regardless, and flag if `/app/` unexpectedly does carry restrictive CSP headers.
  - _Why not automatable:_ requires a real browser's DevTools Console (headless vitest/Playwright cannot substitute for human visual/console inspection of a live SSE-driven hot-swap plus CSP header behavior on the actually-shared `/app/` route); the `:34115` wails-dev bridge is the desktop app's own webview, not an independent browser client.
  - _Source:_ Phase 168-02 (#112, v4.2) RESEARCH Pitfall 1 (T-168-03 CSP gap, upstream issue #123) + `WebShareSessionView.plugin-config.test.tsx` traceability row (FIX-01).

- **M-44** Toolbar Share state live-sync check (UX-02, #115; Portty 04-05: the popover switch replaces the share dialog toggle): the toolbar button and the sidebar icon must track the session's real share state.
  1. Open the Share popover from the toolbar and turn the switch on. Confirm the button reads **Shared** on the accent fill live (no reload) and the sidebar row shows its Shared icon.
  2. Turn it off from the popover, then on again from the sidebar row menu (Share), then off with `portty unshare <name>` in a terminal. After each step, within one poll, the button and the row icon agree with the daemon; no stale "Shared" is left behind.
  - _Why not automatable:_ no test mounts `App.tsx` with a live daemon; each seam (`SharePopover` `onSharedChange`, `setSessionWebEnabled`, the sessions poll, the toolbar label) has its own test.
  - _Source:_ Phase 168-08 (#115, v4.2) gap closure; `SharePopover.test.tsx`/`App.test.tsx` traceability rows (UX-02, SHAR-01).

### Category Z — Web-Share, Remote-Viewer & Windowing Bug Fixes (BUG-01..04)

- **M-48** Live mobile-viewport readability floor (BUG-01, #128): the 80-col grid stays legible on a real narrow phone viewport instead of shrinking to unreadable text.
  1. Start a local session as host and enable web-share; copy the share URL.
  2. Open the share URL on a real phone (or a real browser's device-emulation mode at a narrow width, e.g. 375px) — the URL redirects (Phase 159) to `/app/?session=…&cap=…`.
  3. Confirm the terminal text stays legible — the guest scale clamps at the `DEFAULT_GUEST_MIN_SCALE=0.7` readability floor (`computeGuestViewport`) instead of continuing to shrink; confirm the container gains horizontal scroll (`.terminal-guest--scroll-x`, `overflow-x: auto`) once the floor is hit, rather than clipping content.
  4. Confirm the host PTY grid (cols/rows) is unaffected by the phone viewport (guest-only clamp; no `sendResize` from the guest path, D-03 invariant).
  - _Why not automatable:_ jsdom performs no real layout — `clientWidth`/`clientHeight` are always 0, so a real narrow-viewport visual legibility check cannot be asserted in vitest. The floor-clamp math (`computeGuestViewport`) and the guest/host CSS-class isolation are unit-tested; only the on-screen legibility on a genuine narrow viewport is manual.
  - _Source:_ Phase 175-03 (BUG-01, #128); 175-03-SUMMARY.md D4 (deferred live-verification rationale); 175-VALIDATION.md Manual-Only Verifications.

- **M-49** Live owner-ends-session disconnect notice (BUG-02, #125): a guest sees an accessible disconnect banner, not a frozen terminal, when the owner stops the shared session.
  1. Start a local session as host, enable web-share, and open the share URL as a guest in a real browser (or a second desktop client as a remote guest).
  2. On the owner's machine, stop/end the shared session (kill the session, or toggle web-share off).
  3. Confirm the guest's terminal does NOT go silently frozen. Confirm a visible disconnect banner appears with the fixed text "Session ended — the owner stopped this session.", `role="status"`, and `aria-live="polite"` (screen-reader announced).
  4. Confirm the banner never displays raw server-side close-reason text or any session-owner-controlled string — only the fixed generic copy.
  5. Confirm no auto-reconnect occurs (the banner is dismiss-only; dismissing it does not reopen the connection) — this is the intended phase-locked behavior, not a bug.
  - _Why not automatable:_ requires a live daemon + a real WebSocket session-end event delivered to a real browser guest; the accessibility contract (`role="status"`/`aria-live="polite"`) and the hostile-string injection guard are unit-tested in `SessionEndedBanner.test.tsx`, but the live owner-ends-session → guest-sees-banner integration (and the absence of a frozen/silent terminal) requires human observation.
  - _Source:_ Phase 175-06 (BUG-02, #125); 175-06-SUMMARY.md D4 rationale (`human_judgment: true`); 175-VALIDATION.md Manual-Only Verifications.

- **M-50** BUG-03 (#126) / TERM-05 exited-session tab-close — standing regression watch:
  - Portty 01-10 replaced the per-session exit poll (300 s deadline, only for sessions created in the current GUI run) with one app-wide exit watcher in `app.go` (`startExitWatcher`/`checkExits`, 500 ms, no deadline). Every stopped session the daemon lists produces exactly one `session:exit`, and the GUI closes its tab and removes the session. `175-01-DIAGNOSIS.md` (VERDICT: DISPROVED) remains the history for the original report.
  1. In the built app, start a session, run `sleep 400; exit 0`, and leave it. Confirm the tab closes on exit, with no toast or banner, even though the shell lived more than 5 minutes.
  2. If a user reports a tab that stays open after its shell exited, confirm the daemon still lists the session (`portty list`) and whether it reads stopped. A stopped session with an open tab means the watcher or the frontend handler failed; capture the app log around the failure.
  - _Why not automatable:_ needs the native webview, a live daemon and real wall-clock time past 5 minutes. The watcher logic itself is covered by `app_exit_test.go`.
  - _Source:_ 175-01-DIAGNOSIS.md; Portty 01-10 (CONTEXT Amendment 2, RESEARCH Pitfall 12).

- **M-51** Live two-client alt-screen reconnect (BUG-04, #119 Problem 2): a late-joining or reconnecting guest sees the current screen, not a blank/garbled window, after the raw scrollback ring has wrapped past alt-screen entry.
  1. Start a local session as host and run a full-screen TUI agent (e.g. `htop`, `vim`, or an interactive coding agent) long enough that the raw 256 KiB scrollback ring wraps past the `ESC[?1049h` alt-screen-enter sequence (generate enough terminal output/redraws).
  2. Join as a guest (web-share URL, or a remote peer opening the session in an in-app tab) AFTER the wrap has occurred, and/or force a guest reconnect (close and reopen the tab/browser).
  3. Confirm the guest sees the CURRENT live screen content in the correct alt-screen mode — not a blank window, not garbled/stale content, and not the pre-alt-screen scrollback.
  4. Repeat with the host exiting the alt-screen TUI (returning to the normal buffer) and reconnecting a guest again; confirm the guest correctly reflects normal-buffer mode.
  - _Why not automatable:_ requires a live daemon, a real long-running full-screen TUI to force an actual ring wrap, and a late-joining/reconnecting guest exercising the real timing — the emulator/`RenderSnapshot()` reconstruction logic is unit-tested in `internal/relay/scrollback_altscreen_test.go`, but the live end-to-end wrap-then-reconnect race is not.
  - _Source:_ Phase 175-04 (BUG-04, #119 Problem 2); 175-04-SUMMARY.md D4 rationale (`human_judgment: true`); 175-VALIDATION.md Manual-Only Verifications.

### Category AA — Platform & Hardening Bug Fixes (BUG-05..07)

- **M-53** Production-build `/app/` CSP console sweep (BUG-06, #123, D-06): confirm the newly-added `ws.cspHeaders` wrap on `/app/` (176-02) does not break the public guest SPA in a real browser.
  1. Produce a production build: `wails build -tags "webkit2_41,wailsassets"` (see M-34 for the same build-tag precedent — `/app/` 503s under `wails dev`/plain `go build` since no SPA bundle is embedded).
  2. Load `/app/` (directly, or through a share link's redirect) and confirm the SPA renders normally.
  3. Open the browser DevTools console and confirm there is no Content-Security-Policy violation that breaks functionality — check inline scripts, `wasm-unsafe-eval`, `connect-src` (SSE + WS relay), `font-src`, and `img-src data:` in particular (the same policy already proven clean on `/dashboard` and `/sessions/{id}`).
  4. If a genuine violation is found, relax ONLY that specific directive (D-07 — no blanket loosening); otherwise no action needed.
  - _Why not automatable:_ requires a production Vite build (not `wails dev`) and a real browser DevTools console read — `TestCSPHeaderStrict_App` proves the header is present on a 200 response, but not that the live SPA is violation-free in an actual browser. This item is OPPORTUNISTIC (release-time), not ship-blocking.
  - _Source:_ Phase 176-02 (BUG-06, #123); 176-02-SUMMARY.md D2 rationale (`human_judgment: true`).

### Category AB — Tailscale Connection Check (NET-01)

- **M-57** NET-01 live Tailscale check (Portty 01-12, CONTEXT Amendments 1 and 3): Portty only reads the connection state from the LocalAPI and never installs or configures Tailscale.
  1. Portty and the upstream app use separate sockets and ports since Portty 03-01/03-02, so nothing needs stopping first. Launch the Portty build. Open two sessions; have a second Mac on the tailnet with a session of its own.
  2. Disconnect Tailscale from its menu bar app. Within 10 seconds (or immediately when the Portty window regains focus) the banner reads exactly "Tailscale isn't connected. Remote access and sharing are off until it is." and has no dismiss button.
  3. Confirm the toolbar Share button is dimmed (`aria-disabled`, still focusable) and does nothing when clicked, no peer groups show in the sidebar, Settings ▸ Sharing shows "Not connected" on the Tailscale row with that sentence under it, and both terminals keep working.
  4. Reconnect Tailscale and click back into the Portty window. The banner disappears on focus (or within 10 seconds), Share re-enables, Settings shows "Connected" via the tailnet domain or IP, and peer groups return, all without a restart. At no point does Portty offer to install Tailscale.
  - _Why not automatable:_ needs a real Tailscale client to toggle, a second tailnet device and the native webview's focus events. The decision logic is unit-tested in `internal/webserver/tailscale_test.go`, the emit-on-change poller in `app_test.go`, the share refusal while not connected (`TestShare_TailscaleOff`) in `internal/daemon/share_test.go`, the UI in `TailscaleBanner.test.tsx`, `SettingsSheet.tailscale-status.test.tsx` and `App.tailscale.test.tsx`.
  - _Source:_ 01-12-PLAN.md Task 2 human-check; 01-CONTEXT.md Amendment 3 (Start Web Server gate); REQUIREMENTS NET-01.

### Category AC — Window Chrome & Menu Bar (LOOK-01)

- **M-58** LOOK-01 hidden-inset window chrome (Portty 02-01 Task 1 human check). Ships `mac.TitleBarHiddenInset()`; the `mac.TitleBarHidden()` fallback was not needed (click-tested on macOS 27.2).
  1. Portty and the upstream app use separate sockets and ports since Portty 03-01/03-02, so nothing needs stopping first. Open the built app with two sessions open.
  2. Top-left corner: the native traffic lights sit over the sidebar's top strip and no window title shows.
  3. Click each tab in the 52px top bar, a tab's ×, and the new-tab control: each acts on the first click and none starts a window drag.
  4. Click the sidebar's Hide Sidebar button at the right end of its top strip, then the tab bar's Show Sidebar button: the sidebar hides and shows. Hidden, the first tab starts clear of the traffic lights (80px inset; Portty 02-04, see M-61).
  5. Drag empty top-bar space and the empty sidebar strip: the window moves, and no sidebar label gets text-selected.
  6. Vibrancy: put a colourful wallpaper or window behind the app. The sidebar shows a translucent desktop tint; the tab bar and terminal stay opaque and readable. (Not verifiable in the Tart VM, where sidebar pixels sample identical with or without content behind; check on a real host.)
  - _Why not automatable:_ native NSToolbar hit-testing, native window drag and NSVisualEffectView vibrancy are outside vitest, Go tests and Playwright. The window flags are unit-tested in `menu_test.go` (`TestMacWindowOptions`) and the CSS in `App.test.tsx`.
  - _Source:_ 02-01-PLAN.md Task 1 human-check; 02-UI-SPEC.md "Window chrome contract".
- **M-59** LOOK-01 menu bar (Portty 02-01 Task 2).
  1. ⌘T (and File ▸ New Session) opens the New Session dialog.
  2. ⌘, (and Portty ▸ Settings…) opens Settings.
  3. Help ▸ Portty Help opens https://portty.app in the default browser.
  4. ⌘H hides the app.
  5. ⌘Q shows the quit-confirm dialog (it does not quit straight away).
  6. ⌘W closes the active tab without ending its session (Portty 02-08, M-63). ⌃⌘S hides and shows the sidebar (Portty 02-04, M-61).
  - _Why not automatable:_ native NSMenu accelerators and Wails event delivery need the built app. Menu structure, accelerators and emitted events are unit-tested in `menu_test.go`; the frontend listeners in `App.test.tsx`.
  - _Source:_ 02-01-PLAN.md Task 2; 02-UI-SPEC.md "Interaction Contract ▸ Menu bar".

### Category AD — Live Light/Dark (THEM-01)

- **M-60** THEM-01 live appearance switch (Portty 02-03 Task 3).
  1. In the built app open two terminal tabs: one with WebGL on, one after turning WebGL off in Settings. Run `ls -la /usr/bin` in each and leave one tab hidden. Open Settings.
  2. Toggle System Settings ▸ Appearance Light → Dark → Light. Each toggle repaints the chrome, both terminals and Settings within a second, with no restart and no garbled or overlapping glyphs. Selecting the hidden tab shows the new palette at once. No dark surface remains in Light mode.
  3. Settings ▸ Appearance reads "Automatic" with "Follows your Mac's Light or Dark setting in System Settings." and has no theme switch or theme list.
  4. Open the web share link of a shared session in Safari and toggle macOS appearance again: the browser terminal follows.
  5. The menu bar tray glyph stays legible in both modes; an OSC 9;4 run (`printf '\e]9;4;1;50\a'`) shows a legible progress indicator in both modes.
  - _Why not automatable:_ live NSAppearance propagation into WKWebView, WebGL atlas repaint and tray template rendering only happen in the native app. The palettes, the hook and the token blocks are unit-tested (`terminalPalettes.test.ts`, `useColorScheme.test.tsx`, `style.tokens.test.ts`); the browser switch is covered in Chromium by `web-color-scheme.spec.ts`.
  - _Source:_ 02-03-PLAN.md Task 3; 02-UI-SPEC.md "Color" and "Terminal palettes (THEM-01)".

### Category AE — Sidebar & Navigation (NAV-01..03)

- **M-61** NAV-01..03 sidebar in the built app (Portty 02-04 Task 3 human check).
  1. Launch the app: it opens straight into sidebar plus tabs, and the only tabs are session tabs; running sessions come back as tabs.
  2. Run `portty new ~` from a terminal: the session appears under "This Mac" within 3 s. Clicking a row focuses its tab, or opens one at the end of the strip; the row turns blue (selected).
  3. Press ⌃⌘S twice, then click the sidebar's Hide Sidebar button and the tab bar's Show Sidebar button: the sidebar hides and shows each time, and while hidden the first tab sits clear of the traffic lights (80px inset). Relaunch: the sidebar is shown again (not persisted).
  4. With a row selected, switch to Finder: the selected row turns gray; switch back: it turns blue again.
  5. Vibrancy: with a colourful wallpaper behind the window, the sidebar is translucent and its text (rows, group headers, footer) is readable in both Light and Dark mode. In Dark with the window frontmost, the sidebar is the native Sidebar material (dark and tinted by what is behind it, like Finder's), not a flat mid-grey; with another app frontmost it goes flat, as Finder's does (quick 261115-rc8).
  6. Footer: "Tailscale connected · <this Mac's short name>" with a green dot; disconnect Tailscale: "Tailscale not connected" with a gray dot, and the peer area reads "Peers appear when Tailscale is connected."
  - _Why not automatable:_ native window key status, NSVisualEffectView vibrancy (the Sidebar material set by `appearance_darwin.m` `setSidebarMaterial`, applied from `App.startup`), the native menu accelerator and a CLI-created session against the live daemon only exist in the built app. Rows, states, footer and wiring are covered by `Sidebar.test.tsx`, `sidebarPeers.test.ts`, `App.nav.test.tsx` and `TabBar.test.tsx`.
  - _Source:_ 02-04-PLAN.md Task 3 human-check; 02-UI-SPEC.md "Sidebar" and "Interaction Contract".

- **M-69** Sidebar native, alive refresh (quick 261116-c9f), checked in the Tart VM `portty-test` in Light and Dark.
  1. Header glyphs show before "This Mac" and "Other Macs".
  2. The "+" appears on hovering or Tab-focusing the This Mac header, and stays visible with no sessions.
  3. With no sessions, This Mac shows the accent "New Session ⌘T" row. Tab reaches it, and Enter or a click opens the New Session dialog, as the "+" does.
  4. Unselected rows show a subtle fill on hover. The selected row keeps the blue pill and turns grey when another app is frontmost.
  5. Other Macs shows a host row per peer: a filled green dot when reachable, a hollow grey ring when not. Sessions are indented under it. A reachable idle peer reads "Nothing shared".
  6. The footer is one compact 11px line that truncates, and its tooltip shows the full text. Disconnected, the dot is a hollow grey ring.
  - _Why not automatable:_ jsdom has no `:hover`, layout or vibrancy, and real peers exist only on a live tailnet. The DOM, wiring and copy are covered by `Sidebar.test.tsx`, `sidebarPeers.test.ts`, `App.nav.test.tsx` and `App.open-remote.test.tsx`.
  - _Source:_ `.planning/quick/261116-c9f-sidebar-native-alive-refresh/261116-c9f-PLAN.md`.

### Category AF — Chat Removed (CHAT-01)

- **M-62** CHAT-01 live check (Portty 02-07). Stop any older Portty daemon first (or reboot): a daemon from an older build keeps serving chat routes until it restarts.
  1. Launch the built app and open a local session tab: nothing chat-related shows, and the terminal fills the whole area below the toolbar.
  2. Web-share the session and open the share link in a browser: the page shows only the terminal.
  3. While the browser guest is open, resize the host terminal: the browser terminal re-scales to the host grid (carried over from retired M-31 step 5, Phase 157 scale parity through the `/app/` redirect).
  4. `curl -sk -o /dev/null -w '%{http_code}' https://<host>:<port>/api/chat/<id>/history` returns 404.
  - _Why not automatable:_ the native WebView and a live daemon behind the real tailnet listener exist only in the built app; vitest, Playwright and the Go `chat_removed_test.go` suites cover the pieces.
  - _Source:_ 02-07-PLAN.md Task 2; REQUIREMENTS CHAT-01; RESEARCH Pitfall 12.

### Category AG — Tabs & Toolbar (TERM-03, LOOK-01)

- **M-63** Tabs, detach, End Session, rename and the toolbar Share button (Portty 02-08). Run in Light, then Dark.
  1. Open three tabs. Close the middle one with its ×: its sidebar row stays and the right neighbour becomes active. Press ⌘W until no tab is left: each close activates the right neighbour (else the left), the shells stay in the sidebar, and the empty state "No Open Sessions" shows with a New Session button and ⌘T. ⌘W with no tabs does nothing. Reopen a session from the sidebar: the terminal still has its history.
  2. Web-share one session, then choose End Session… from its tab's right-click menu: the confirm reads "End “<name>”?" with the extra line "Anyone attached from another Mac or a browser will be disconnected.", and Cancel has focus. Confirm: the tab and the sidebar row disappear and the share link stops working.
  3. Rename a session by double-clicking its tab name, then another by double-clicking its sidebar row (and once with the Rename menu item): each new name shows in both the tab and the row at once. Esc cancels; an empty name reverts.
  4. Open 15 tabs: they shrink to 88px, then the strip scrolls and the Scroll tabs left/right buttons appear; New Session and Share never scroll away. Close all but one: the single tab is one left-aligned pill no wider than 220px. A long name truncates with an ellipsis, clear of the close button.
  5. Click Share: the Share popover opens under the button (M-81 covers its look). Turn sharing on: the button reads "Shared" on the accent fill. Open a peer session tab: an "On <host>" chip replaces Share. Turn Tailscale off and Tab to the Share button: it is dimmed, keeps its name and does nothing.
  6. Hide the sidebar: the first tab sits clear of the traffic lights (80px inset), and dragging the toolbar's empty space moves the window while tabs and buttons still click.
  - _Why not automatable:_ native layout in WKWebView, VoiceOver's reading of `aria-describedby`/`aria-disabled`, window dragging and a real daemon's detach/kill behaviour exist only in the built app. The logic is covered by `tabs.test.ts`, `TabBar.test.tsx`, `ContextMenu.test.tsx`, `Sidebar.test.tsx` and `App.test.tsx`.
  - _Source:_ 02-08-PLAN.md Task 3 human-check; REQUIREMENTS TERM-03, LOOK-01; 02-UI-SPEC "Toolbar and tabs", "Share button (toolbar)".

### Category AH — Dialogs and Sheets (LOOK-01)

- **M-64** Every dialog in the sheet style (Portty 02-09). In the built app, open each dialog in Light, then Dark: New Session (⌘T), End Session… (tab menu), Join Remote Session (a peer row with no cached cap), Regenerate Signing Key and the disable-warning confirm (Settings), Quit (⌘Q with sessions running), and also the link confirm popover (⌘-click an OSC 8 link whose text and target differ).
  1. Each dialog attaches under the toolbar, centred on the window, on the window colour with a thin border, 12px corners and a soft shadow, over a light (Light) or darker (Dark) scrim. No dark-only dialog remains in Light mode. The link confirm popover uses the same colours at the click point.
  2. No dialog has a header ×. Buttons sit bottom-right, the primary on the accent fill; End Session's confirm is red.
  3. Focus starts on the first input (Join), the primary button (New Session) or Cancel (End Session, Regenerate, Quit). Tab and Shift+Tab stay inside the dialog. Esc cancels. A scrim click does nothing. On close, focus returns to the control that opened it.
  4. The dialog fades in with a small downward slide. With System Settings ▸ Accessibility ▸ Display ▸ Reduce motion on, dialogs appear and disappear with no animation.
  5. If creating a session fails (try a folder deleted after choosing it; whether that fails depends on the daemon), the New Session dialog stays open with "Couldn't create the session: … Check that the folder exists, then try again." in red.
  - _Why not automatable:_ the WKWebView rendering of the light/dark tokens, the native `<select>`, and the reduced-motion system setting exist only in the built app. The focus and dismissal logic is covered by `useDialogFocus.test.tsx` and the dialog tests listed under LOOK-01 in Section 4.
  - _Source:_ 02-09-PLAN.md Task 2; REQUIREMENTS LOOK-01; 02-UI-SPEC "Dialogs and sheets", "Share dialog".

### Category AI — Banners (LOOK-01, NET-01)

- **M-65** Every banner in the shared style (Portty 02-10). In the built app, in Light, then Dark, trigger each banner: Tailscale off (quit the Tailscale app), a WebGL software fallback if reproducible, an update notice from a fake feed, a plugin toggle (Settings: Unicode 11 or Inline Images, then save), a save error (Save Terminal As… to an unwritable folder), and the daemon error (start with the daemon unreachable).
  1. Banners sit under the toolbar in the order Tailscale, WebGL recovery, update, plugin toggle, save feedback. Each is one thin strip (8px 16px padding, 12px text) on the yellow warning tokens (Tailscale, WebGL, save error, daemon error) or the blue info tokens (update, plugin toggle, save notice), with no dark-only banner left in Light mode.
  2. Each shows a 16px outline icon (triangle for warning, circled "i" for info), never a ⚠ glyph. Dismissible banners close with a 24×24 × icon button labelled "Dismiss"; the Tailscale banner has none.
  3. With Tailscale off, "Open Tailscale" opens the Tailscale app. With the app not installed (or renamed), it opens https://tailscale.com/download/mac in the browser. It never installs or configures anything.
  - _Why not automatable:_ the WKWebView rendering of the light/dark banner tokens and launching the real Tailscale app exist only in the built app on a Mac. The order, variants, icons and dismiss logic are covered by the banner tests and `App.test.tsx`; the fixed argv and fallback by `TestOpenTailscale_*`.
  - _Source:_ 02-10-PLAN.md Task 2; REQUIREMENTS LOOK-01, NET-01; 02-UI-SPEC "Banner".

### Category AJ — Settings Sheet (LOOK-01, THEM-01, NAV-03)

- **M-66** Settings as a native-looking sheet (Portty 02-11). In the built app, in Light, then Dark:
  1. Press ⌘, (or choose Settings… in the app menu). One sheet attaches under the toolbar at 720×560 (smaller on a short window, where only the pane content scrolls and the header and Done stay visible). No Settings tab appears in the tab strip.
  2. The header shows the pane name centred over General / Sharing / Security pane tabs. Switch panes with the mouse, then with ←/→ after focusing a pane tab; the selected tab gets the grey fill and blue icon and label.
  3. General: Appearance reads "Automatic". Toggle "Start hidden in the menu bar": every toggle is a native-looking switch (blue when on, grey when off) that dims while saving. Toggle a plugin and click Save Plugins ("Saving…", "Saved!").
  4. Sharing, with Tailscale off: the Tailscale row reads "Not connected" with the sentence under it, and the pane shows only that row and the explanation "Share a session from the toolbar, or right-click it in the sidebar. …" (Portty 04-05).
  5. Security: click "I Understand"; the row then shows "Acknowledged" in green. Click "Regenerate…": the confirm opens over the sheet with "Keep Links" focused and a red "Invalidate All Links"; Esc closes only the confirm.

### Category AO — Terminal Font and First Prompt (LOOK-01, TERM-01)

- **M-75** Terminal Font live apply (quick 261116-ku9; Tart VM, built app).

  4. Quit and relaunch: the row and the terminals show the saved family and size.

  2. Repeat on the host Mac (WebGL renderer).
